What reaches a human after 65 cases

A 49-case synthetic benchmark, not live Ryde volume. Each count is recomputed from the stored rulings.

Cases in the batch
65

Route deviation, no-show fee and safety scenarios, each with reference outcomes derived from the written policy.

→
Core decisions auto-cleared
42/ 65

23 auto-cleared for voucher issuance ($126.98 in Ryde vouchers) and 19 with no refund due. Each one passed the pre-execution policy check.

→
Sent to a person
23

Each one was stopped by the policy check or escalated by the judge. They arrive with the facts, both arguments and the policy trace already prepared.

Where it plugs in

Inputs

What the court reads

trip · GPS · chat · fare · profiles · OneMap route · LTA traffic · weather

Outputs

What it hands back

cleared / needs a person · proposed actions · plain-language notices · audit receipt

Production connectors (not included)

Where Ryde would connect

  • trip records in → one case record
  • refund → wallet / voucher API
  • account flag → trust and safety queue
  • appeals → audit receipt

Route deviation · B-0002

Detour · consent given beforehand

24.4 s
Dispute statement

The driver took a longer way round than the app showed and I paid more than the estimate. I want the difference back.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Clarke Quay → Bedok Mall, estimate 15.5 km / 21.8 min, pickup 12:14 drop-off 12:46; charged $41.09 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Human review

Send to a human reviewer: confirm from the path record whether the 12:29–12:39 off-route segment (F3) was actually driven on Sims Avenue, the road the rider agreed to (F8, F9). If it was, no refund (RD-5); if not, refund 3.27 km × $1.4098/km = $4.61 (RD-2), and decide separately on the $0.11 metered waiting unit (F5, RD-3(c)).

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 19.4 km vs optimal route 15.5 km: +3.9 km (+25%).
  • F2 Trip duration 32.2 min vs estimated 21.8 min (+10.4 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 12:29–12:39: 6.34 km driven where the optimal route covers 3.07 km, adding 3.27 km (up to 755 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $5.54 (3.93 km × $1.4098/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Waiting fee billed $0.11 (0.4 min × $0.26/min).
  • F6 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F7 Total charged $41.09 SGD: base $3.90, distance $27.35 (19.40 km), time $8.26 (31.8 min), waiting $0.11, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F8 Chat #1 12:26 driver: “Traffic ahead on Pan Island Expressway is building up, ok if I go round via Sims Avenue?”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The GPS record shows one off-route segment, 12:29–12:39, in which the car covered 6.34 km where the optimal route covers 3.07 km, adding 3.27 km (F3); this is a detour segment under RD-1 and was not exempted.
  • The segment began at 12:29 while the rider's only message, 'Sure, go ahead.' (F9), was sent at 12:27, so the rider spoke before the segment; the question is only what her words reach.
Driver's advocate
  • This is a RydeTAXI metered trip; the rider's complaint is that the driver took a longer way round, and the disputed driving is the single off-route segment 12:29–12:39 that added 3.27 km (F3), against an overall excess of 3.9 km over the 15.5 km optimal route (F1).
  • Before the off-route segment began (start 12:29), the driver asked at 12:26 whether he could 'go round via Sims Avenue' because traffic on the Pan Island Expressway was building up, and the rider replied at 12:27 'Sure, go ahead.' - a rider message, sent by the rider herself before the segment, agreeing to the named road Sims Avenue (F8, F9, F10).
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 12:29:55 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F8: Chat #1 12:26 driver: “Traffic ahead on Pan Island Expressway is building up, ok if I go round via Sims Avenue?”; F9: Chat #2 12:27 rider: “Sure, go ahead.”; F11: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “Traffic was building up ahead so I asked the rider before turning off via Sims Avenue and the rider agreed.” (F8, F9, F11)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F8, F9)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F17)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4098/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why automation stopped

segment finding undetermined for F3; evidence unknown: Whether the driven 12:29–12:39 segment was actually on Sims Avenue: the record carries no road name for the driven path, no message or stop placing the car on Sims Avenue, and the only naming of Sims Avenue is the driver's post-trip statement, which cannot establish the tie.

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the record does not settle whether the rider's agreement covers this segment; a person decides whether $4.61 is refunded.
  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.
Notices
Notice to the rider
Your dispute on trip B-0002 has been passed to a Ryde reviewer. No decision has been made yet: nothing has been refunded or charged as part of this review. The reviewer will look at what was agreed about the route and which stretch it covered; the trip record, including the parts that are missing from it. Ryde will contact you once the review is complete.
Notice to the driver
The dispute on trip B-0002 has been passed to a Ryde reviewer. No decision has been made yet, and nothing changes on your account or earnings as part of this review. The reviewer will look at what was agreed about the route and which stretch it covered; the trip record, including the parts that are missing from it. Ryde will contact you once the review is complete.

Route deviation · B-0010

Detour · rider named a stop

38.3 s
Dispute statement

I asked to stop at Paya Lebar Square and we did, fine, but the fare was much more than the estimate. I think the detour was overcharged.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Clarke Quay → Bedok Mall, estimate 15.5 km / 21.8 min, pickup 12:10 drop-off 12:43; charged $40.74 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Human review

Send to a human reviewer: confirm whether the Paya Lebar Square approach (segment 12:25–12:36) is covered by the rider's RD-3(a) named stop; if the way there is exempt, close with no_action (waiting fee $1.15 stands under RD-3(c)); if the contested drift is not consent, refund $4.19 as a voucher. No account flag (RD-6 unmet, F18).

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 19.0 km vs optimal route 15.5 km: +3.5 km (+22%).
  • F2 Stationary 4 min 0 s at (1.3175, 103.8922), 12:28–12:32. Not a pickup or drop-off point (5890 m from pickup, 4243 m from drop-off).
  • F3 Trip duration 33.2 min vs estimated 21.8 min (+11.4 min).
  • F4 Off-route segment 12:25–12:36: 4.57 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.60 km, adding 2.97 km (up to 729 m from the optimal route).
  • F5 Excess distance billed $4.91 (3.48 km × $1.4100/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F6 Waiting fee billed $1.15 (4.4 min × $0.26/min).
  • F7 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F8 Total charged $40.74 SGD: base $3.90, distance $26.72 (18.95 km), time $7.50 (28.8 min), waiting $1.15, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The GPS record shows one off-route segment, 12:25–12:36, in which the car drove 4.57 km where the optimal route covers 1.60 km, adding 2.97 km (F4), and the trip overall ran 19.0 km against an optimal 15.5 km, +3.5 km / +22% (F1). This is an unexempted detour segment under RD-1.
  • The rider's consent reaches only a stop: at 12:21 the rider wrote “Can you stop at Paya Lebar Square? I need to grab a parcel, 3-4 minutes.” (F9) and the driver answered “Ok can” (F10), i.e. agreement to a short named stop, not to a routing change; the driver's own statement says only that he drove there and waited (F12).
Driver's advocate
  • The rider herself named the stop before it began: F9 (12:21) asks 'Can you stop at Paya Lebar Square? I need to grab a parcel, 3-4 minutes.' and F10 (12:21) is the driver's acceptance, both timestamped before the off-route segment that starts at 12:25 (F4).
  • The car did stop where the rider asked: F2 records a stationary period of 4 min 0 s at 12:28–12:32 at a point that is neither pickup nor drop-off, which is the parcel stop requested in F9; the duration matches the rider's own stated 3–4 minutes.

  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F4] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F4] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 12:25:20 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F9: Chat #1 12:21 rider: “Can you stop at Paya Lebar Square? I need to grab a parcel, 3-4 minutes.”; F10: Chat #2 12:21 driver: “Ok can”; F12: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “The rider asked me to stop at Paya Lebar Square, so I drove there and waited while the rider went in.” (F9, F10, F12)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F4] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. Stops in this segment: F2. (F9, F10, F2)

Route deviation · B-0013

Two detours · first one agreed

33.0 s
Dispute statement

I agreed to go round the jam near the start, but later he went off the route a second time without asking. I want that part refunded.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Woodlands Civic Centre → Orchard Road, estimate 20.6 km / 28.8 min, pickup 18:31 drop-off 19:18; charged $61.32 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Human review

Refer to a human reviewer: (1) determine whether the 18:46–18:56 segment (F3) is tied to Rifle Range Road, and if not, whether the rider's pre-segment "Sure, your call." (F10) authorises the F3 route; (2) issue the refund on the segment(s) found unexcused, computed as extra_km × $1.4100/km, with the F4 4.78 km leg [F4][F5] at least $6.74; (3) apply RD-6 only if a prior upheld route dispute exists in 90 days - on this record route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 [F18]; (4) on the same review, confirm whether any rider message covers the 19:03–19:12 segment [F4][F9][F10][F11].

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 31.0 km vs optimal route 20.6 km: +10.4 km (+50%).
  • F2 Trip duration 47.3 min vs estimated 28.8 min (+18.5 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 18:46–18:56: 7.10 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.43 km, adding 5.66 km (up to 1923 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Off-route segment 19:03–19:12: 5.77 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.00 km, adding 4.78 km (up to 1680 m from the optimal route).
  • F5 Excess distance billed $14.64 (10.38 km × $1.4100/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F6 Waiting fee billed $0.11 (0.4 min × $0.26/min).
  • F7 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F8 Total charged $61.32 SGD: base $3.90, distance $43.64 (30.95 km), time $12.20 (46.9 min), waiting $0.11, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • This is a RydeTAXI metered trip: the rider was charged $61.32 with distance billed $43.64 for 30.95 km and the actual track shows 31.0 km against a 20.6 km optimal route, so extra distance went straight into the metered fare.
  • The GPS record shows two off-route segments: 18:46–18:56 added 5.66 km and 19:03–19:12 added 4.78 km, totalling 10.44 km of unexplained extra distance.
Driver's advocate
  • The trip is a RydeTAXI metered trip (F8), so any refund is limited to the metered mileage actually added by any unexcused segment, calculated at this trip's per-km rate of $1.41 (F5), not to the whole $61.32 fare.
  • Segment F3 (18:46–18:56, +5.66 km) was covered by the rider's own consent: the driver asked before leaving the optimal route, 'ok if I go round via Rifle Range Road?'   • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (F15)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 10:13:30 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F8: Chat #1 10:10 driver: “Looks like traffic on Ayer Rajah Expressway is building up, mind if I go round via Clementi Avenue 2?”; F9: Chat #2 10:10 rider: “Sure, your call.”; F11: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “There was a breakdown on the route, the whole road was slow, so I took another way. Check the LTA incident feed.” (F8, F9, F11)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F8, F9)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F18)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4099/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why automation stopped

segment finding undetermined for F3; evidence insufficient: No record ties the driven off-route path (F3, 10:13–10:24) to any named road or place: F3 carries no road name, F10 shows the chat consists of only two messages, and there is no stop or locating fact within the segment window, so the RD-5 scope of the rider's 10:10 agreement (F9, replying to F8's naming of Clementi Avenue 2) cannot be read from the materials. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Route deviation · B-0018

Detour · repeat offender

23.0 s
Dispute statement

My app estimated 8 km. The driver took some other way and the meter ended at 11 km. He never asked. I want the difference refunded.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Jurong East MRT → NUS, estimate 8.4 km / 13.8 min, pickup 12:06 drop-off 12:22; charged $24.80 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Partial refund $3.00 · account flag → a person

Issue a $3.00 Ryde voucher (NS-7) to the rider for the unexempted off-route segment's extra distance, and send the driver's account to Ryde account review (RD-6) for the repeated route dispute.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 10.7 km vs optimal route 8.4 km: +2.3 km (+27%).
  • F2 Trip duration 16.5 min vs estimated 13.8 min (+2.7 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 12:12–12:16: 2.93 km driven where the optimal route covers 0.80 km, adding 2.13 km (up to 954 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $3.26 (2.31 km × $1.4097/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F6 Total charged $24.80 SGD: base $3.90, distance $15.14 (10.74 km), time $4.29 (16.5 min), waiting $0.00, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F7 No in-app chat messages between rider and driver.
  • F8 Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “I went where the GPS sent me; it rerouted. The fare is what the meter showed.”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The driven route was 10.7 km against an optimal 8.4 km, +2.3 km (+27%), and the meter therefore billed more distance units than the shortest and most direct route would have (F1).
  • The trip recorded one off-route segment, 12:12–12:16, in which 2.93 km were driven where the optimal route covers 0.80 km, adding 2.13 km and leaving the optimal route by up to 954 m (F3); this segment is not covered by any exemption, so it is an unexempted deviation under RD-1.
Driver's advocate
  • The trip is RydeTAXI and metered (F6), so the fare follows the meter rather than the app's 8.4 km quote, which means only meter distance units attributable to an unexcused segment can be refunded, not the whole gap between the quoted and actual distance.
  • There is exactly one off-route segment on the record: 12:12–12:16, 2.93 km driven where the optimal route covers 0.80 km, adding 2.13 km and straying up to 954 m from the optimal route (F3); the +2.3 km overall excess in F1 is therefore essentially this single segment, not a trip-long pattern of detours.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ✗ RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour [F3] no rider message on record before 12:12:15; the driver's statement is a claim, not consent (G11) (F8)
  • ✗ RD-3 rider-requested stop [F3] no rider message before 12:12:15 names a place (F8)
  • ✓ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 2 (F14)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4097/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: True
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance. The account flag is not cleared: it goes to a person in the trust and safety queue.

What would change this ruling
  • Had an LTA incident been recorded on the stretch F3 replaced, before it started, RD-4 would exempt it (−$3.00).
Notices
Notice to the rider
Good news: you're getting $3.00 back for this trip, issued as a Ryde voucher.

We reviewed the route and found one stretch where the driver went well off the direct way, adding about 2.1 km beyond the best route. Nothing excuses that stretch - there was no recorded traffic incident there, and no message from you agreeing to a different route beforehand.

Because RydeTAXI runs on the meter, we refund only that extra distance, not the whole difference between the app estimate and the meter. Waiting time isn't refundable.

Your voucher is on its way. No action needed from you.
Notice to the driver
We reviewed this trip and found that one stretch of the route was not the direct way, adding about 2.1 km beyond the best route. Because RydeTAXI is metered, that extra distance isn't chargeable, so $3.00 goes back to the rider as a Ryde voucher.

Nothing exempted that stretch on this trip: no traffic incident was recorded on the road you took, and the rider sent no message agreeing to a different route. A navigation reroute alone doesn't exempt it.

Your account is also going to account review, given repeated route disputes. A reviewer will look at your recent trips. You don't need to do anything now.

What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The GPS record shows the driver covered 15.2 km against a 10.7 km optimal route, +4.5 km (+42%), and 23.2 min against an estimated 17.3 min (+5.9 min).
  • One off-route segment is recorded: 14:19-14:26, 5.03 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.39 km, adding 3.64 km and straying up to 1160 m from the optimal route, an unexplained deviation under RD-1.
Driver's advocate
  • The trip was a RydeX fixed-quote service: the fare of $24.93 is composed only of the quoted components (base $3.90, distance $15.06, time $4.50, waiting $0.00, platform $1.47) and the billed distance of 10.7 km does not exceed the optimal route of 10.7 km, so no money is attributable to the off-route segment.
  • The only off-route segment (14:19–14:26) added 3.64 km of driving, but on a non-metered RydeX booking mileage does not change the fare, so the recoverable amount under RD-2 is zero and the correct outcome is no_action.

21.7 s
Dispute statement

The driver marked arrived but was never at the pickup point. I waited there the whole time and then got charged $6.61 as a no-show.

Trip summary

RydeX Bugis Junction → Tanjong Pagar Centre, accepted 12:02:00, cancelled by driver at 12:09:25; cancellation fee $6.61

Proposed outcome

Full refund $6.61 · account flag → a person

Refund the $6.61 no-show cancellation fee to the rider as a Ryde voucher and send the driver's account for review of the "I'm Here" tap made 600 m from the pickup + flag_account

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Driver's closest approach to pickup: 599 m at 12:06:55. Driver never came within 200 m.
  • F2 Driver stationary 12:05:55–12:09:25 (3 min 30 s) at (1.3048, 103.8549), 600 m from pickup.
  • F3 Accepted 12:02:00. Cancelled by driver at 12:09:25 (7 min 25 s after acceptance). Cancellation fee on record: $6.61.
  • F4 driver_arrived_at is null: the platform never recorded the driver at the pickup point.
  • F5 Driver tapped "I'm Here" at 12:06:15, 600 m from the pickup point at that moment; stayed within 200 m for 0 s after the tap.

  • F7 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F8 Chat #1 12:06 driver: “I'm here already, silver Toyota Altis, please come out”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The driver never reached the pickup point: his closest approach was 599 m and he was never within 200 m of Bugis Junction (F1). He therefore was not at the pick-up location according to GPS data when he tapped "I'm Here" at 12:06:15, which was 600 m away, and he stayed within 200 m for 0 s after the tap (F5).
  • The platform itself never recorded the driver arriving: driver_arrived_at is null (F4). The only arrival indication is the driver's own tap, made 600 m from the pickup (F5), so condition (ii) of NS-1 fails and the $6.61 no-show fee cannot stand.
Driver's advocate
  • The system records show the driver did press "I'm Here" for this trip (F5, tap at 12:06:15), so the cancellation was processed as a rider no-show under the arrival-button process rather than as an unexplained driver cancellation.
  • The driver's own records show a genuine wait at a fixed spot: he was stationary for 3 min 30 s from 12:05:55 to 12:09:25 (F2), and his written account states he pressed "I'm Here" and waited more than three minutes before cancelling (F12).

  • ✗ NS-1 (iii) stayed at the pickup more than 3 min after the arrival confirmation, until cancelling (NS-1 default: more than 3 min) [case] 0 s continuously inside the radius after the driver_tap at 12:06:15; threshold 180 s (>) (F5)

  • flag_account required: True
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance. The account flag is not cleared: it goes to a person in the trust and safety queue.

26.7 sReview · Replay
Route deviation · B-0001

Detour · unexplained loop

21.7 s
Dispute statement

The app estimated 21 km; the driver turned off the route without a word and the meter read 24 km at the end. Please refund the extra distance.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Raffles Place → Changi Airport T3, estimate 21.2 km / 28.4 min, pickup 09:07 drop-off 09:43; charged $49.37 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Partial refund $4.33

Refund $4.33 to the rider as a Ryde voucher (NS-7) for the unexempted off-route segment on this metered RydeTAXI trip; record the RD-1 breach; no account flag.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 24.4 km vs optimal route 21.2 km: +3.2 km (+15%).
  • F2 Trip duration 36.8 min vs estimated 28.4 min (+8.4 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 09:24–09:30: 3.78 km driven where the optimal route covers 0.71 km, adding 3.07 km (up to 688 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $4.51 (3.20 km × $1.4101/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F6 Total charged $49.37 SGD: base $3.90, distance $34.42 (24.41 km), time $9.58 (36.8 min), waiting $0.00, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F7 Chat #1 09:04 rider: “Hi, I am at the Raffles Place taxi stand, with a black backpack.”
  • F8 Chat #2 09:05 driver: “2 min away, blue Toyota Prius.”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The trip is a RydeTAXI metered job where distance is billed per km, so the kilometres driven enter the fare: F6 shows the total charged $49.37 including a distance component of $34.42 for 24.41 km.
  • There is exactly one off-route segment: 09:24–09:30, 3.78 km driven where the optimal route covers 0.71 km, adding 3.07 km and straying up to 688 m from the optimal route (F3). The whole-trip figures - 24.4 km actual vs 21.2 km optimal, +3.2 km (+15%), and 36.8 min vs 28.4 min estimated (F1, F2) - match that single segment, so there is no unexplained residue.
Driver's advocate
  • The ride was a metered RydeTAXI trip, so the record on the fare is the meter itself: the total charged was $49.37 SGD, of which distance $34.42 for 24.41 km and time $9.58 for 36.8 min, and the only distance element the rider disputes is the $4.51 attributed to 3.20 km of excess distance.
  • The record shows exactly one off-route segment, 09:24-09:30, in which 3.78 km were driven where the optimal route covers 0.71 km, adding 3.07 km; the +3.2 km in the GPS summary is that same single event, not two or more separate deviations.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 09:24:50 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F7: Chat #1 09:04 rider: “Hi, I am at the Raffles Place taxi stand, with a black backpack.”; F8: Chat #2 09:05 driver: “2 min away, blue Toyota Prius.”; F10: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “I went where the GPS sent me; it rerouted. The fare is what the meter showed.” (F7, F8, F10)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F7, F8)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F16)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4101/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance.

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the judge found no rider agreement covering this segment, so $4.33 is refunded; a rider message agreeing to it before it started would have exempted it.
  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.
Notices
Notice to the rider
We have reviewed your dispute: part of the fare is refunded. You will receive $4.33 as a Ryde voucher. The decision follows Ryde's dispute policy and the trip record.
Notice to the driver
We reviewed the route on this metered RydeTAXI trip and found one unexempted deviation: about 09:24–09:30 you drove roughly 3 km further than the best route, straying from it as well. No rider message asked for that route and none agreed to it before you took it, and your note that the GPS rerouted you doesn't count as rider consent. No road incident, no slow-traffic speed record, and fair weather applied either. The extra distance is refunded to the rider at your trip's per-km rate: $4.33, issued as a Ryde voucher. No account flag this time.

Proposed outcome

Refund $2.74 (Ryde voucher, NS-7) for the unexempted 1.94 km off-route segment on this RydeTAXI metered trip; record the RD-1 breach on the driver's file (no account flag - RD-6 threshold not met).

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 10.6 km vs optimal route 8.4 km: +2.2 km (+26%).
  • F2 Trip duration 19.0 min vs estimated 13.8 min (+5.2 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 14:28–14:34: 3.07 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.12 km, adding 1.94 km (up to 786 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $3.06 (2.17 km × $1.4104/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Waiting fee billed $0.11 (0.4 min × $0.26/min).
  • F6 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F7 Total charged $25.26 SGD: base $3.90, distance $14.95 (10.60 km), time $4.83 (18.6 min), waiting $0.11, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F8 Chat #1 14:30 driver: “Jam on Ayer Rajah Expressway is building up ahead, can I go round via Clementi Road?”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The trip drove 10.6 km against an optimal 8.4 km (+2.2 km, +26%) and a single off-route segment 14:28–14:34 added 1.94 km, driving 3.07 km where the optimal route covers 1.12 km (F1, F3).
  • That segment is not excused by any rider consent given before it began: the driver's message naming Clementi Road is timestamped 14:30 and the rider's 'Sure, no problem.' is 14:31, while the off-route segment already started at 14:28 (F3, F8, F9, F10).
Driver's advocate
  • The single off-route segment ran 14:28–14:34 and added 1.94 km (3.07 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.12 km), so the disputed detour is bounded at 1.94 km and cannot support the rider's claim to the whole +2.2 km trip excess.
  • Before the segment began, at 14:30, the driver asked 'Jam on Ayer Rajah Expressway is building up ahead, can I go round via Clementi Road?' and the rider answered at 14:31 'Sure, no problem.' - the rider's own written message is route consent, not a mere acknowledgement after the fact, because the segment did not start until 14:28 per F3 but the consent message is the rider's; the rider's contrary framing in the complaint is a statement, not a system record.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ✗ RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour [F3] the rider's message(s) F9 came after the segment started at 14:28:45; the driver's statement is a claim, not consent (G11) (F8, F9, F11)
  • ✗ RD-3 rider-requested stop [F3] no rider message before 14:28:45 names a place (F8, F9, F11)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F18)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4104/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance.

What would change this ruling
  • Had an LTA incident been recorded on the stretch F3 replaced, before it started, RD-4 would exempt it (−$2.74).
  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.
Notices
Notice to the rider
We reviewed your trip and found you were charged for a detour that wasn't agreed to in time. The route taken between 14:28 and 14:34 added about 1.94 km that the most direct route didn't need, and your "sure" came after that stretch had already started - so it can't count as approval.

You'll get $2.74 back as a Ryde voucher. That covers the extra distance only; your waiting charge and the rest of the fare stay as billed. No change to your account.
Notice to the driver
We reviewed the trip and found one stretch of the route was off the shortest path - about 1.94 km between 14:28 and 14:34 that the rider's agreement didn't cover, since it came after the detour began. No traffic incident or road closure explains it.

We're refunding the rider $2.74 as a Ryde voucher for that distance. This breach is recorded on your file. It won't flag your account, but it stays on record and counts if further route disputes are upheld. Keep to the shortest route unless the rider's approval comes first.

  • F7 Chat #1 22:43 rider: “I'm at the kerb outside Mandarin Gallery already, in a grey hoodie.”
  • F8 Chat #2 22:43 driver: “5 min away, grey Kia Niro.”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The ride is a metered RydeTAXI trip (F6 shows a distance-metered breakdown: base $3.90 + distance $18.60 for 13.19 km + time $6.72), so detour kilometres enter the fare directly rather than being absorbed by a fixed quote.

Driver's advocate
  • One off-route segment exists: 22:58–23:05, 3.82 km driven against 1.91 km of optimal route, adding 1.91 km (F3); trip total was 13.2 km against 10.7 km optimal, +2.5 km (F1).
  • The diverting segment started 22:58, and the only rider messages in the record are two 22:43 messages about the pick-up point (F7, F8, F9) - the rider never named a route and never agreed to one before the segment began.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 22:58:50 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F7: Chat #1 22:43 rider: “I'm at the kerb outside Mandarin Gallery already, in a grey hoodie.”; F8: Chat #2 22:43 driver: “5 min away, grey Kia Niro.”; F10: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “Central Expressway was crawling, so I went round it. Any driver would have done the same.” (F7, F8, F10)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F7, F8)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F17)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4102/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why it was cleared

What would change this ruling

  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.

  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F4] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F4] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 14:21:25 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F9: Chat #1 14:17 rider: “Can you stop at Clementi Mall? I need to grab a parcel, 3-4 minutes.”; F10: Chat #2 14:17 driver: “Sure”; F12: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “The rider asked me to stop at Clementi Mall, so I drove there and waited while the rider went in.” (F9, F10, F12)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F4] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. Stops in this segment: F2. (F9, F10, F2)
  • ✓ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 2 (F18)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4100/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: only if a segment is found non-exempt (RD-6 prior upheld dispute on file)
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. No refund is due.

What would change this ruling
  • F4: the judge found the rider's own message covers this segment, so it is exempt; without that message $2.40 would be refunded.
Notices
Notice to the rider
We reviewed your fare dispute and there is no refund. Your trip cost is unchanged.

What decided it: before the driver went off the direct route, you asked to stop at Clementi Mall, and the driver agreed. Because the stop was a place you named, the way the driver reached it isn't treated as a detour, even though the stop took a few minutes. The waiting time on the meter and the booking fee are both chargeable.

So no mileage refund and no fee adjustment are due. No action is being taken on your account, and nothing changes for future trips.
Notice to the driver
We reviewed the fare dispute on your Jurong East MRT to NUS trip and found no issue with your fare. The fare stands as metered.

What decided it: the rider asked you to stop at Clementi Mall before the off-route stretch, and you agreed. A stop the rider names is treated as part of the trip, so reaching it isn't a detour and no mileage refund applies. Your meter waiting time and the platform fee are chargeable.

No action is being taken on your account, and the two route disputes already on your record are not being added to. No further steps are needed from you.

Route deviation · B-0022

Detour · complaint about something else

27.6 s
Dispute statement

I agreed to a quick petrol stop, not to a long loop. The meter was higher than the estimate and he never stopped for petrol anyway.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Toa Payoh HDB Hub → VivoCity, estimate 13.9 km / 20.5 min, pickup 21:34 drop-off 22:02; charged $39.18 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Partial refund $6.43

Issue a Ryde voucher refund of $6.43 to the rider for the unexcused detour segment (RydeTAXI meter excess kilometres), and remind the driver that a rider's agreement to a short stop does not authorise a route change.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 18.7 km vs optimal route 13.9 km: +4.8 km (+35%).
  • F2 Trip duration 28.8 min vs estimated 20.5 min (+8.3 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 21:39–21:49: 6.27 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.71 km, adding 4.56 km (up to 1958 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $6.77 (4.80 km × $1.4100/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Waiting fee billed $0.11 (0.4 min × $0.26/min).
  • F6 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F7 Total charged $39.18 SGD: base $3.90, distance $26.31 (18.66 km), time $7.39 (28.4 min), waiting $0.11, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F8 Chat #1 21:35 driver: “Mind if I pull in at the petrol station just ahead for one minute? Tank is almost empty.”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The trip was 18.7 km against an optimal 13.9 km, i.e. +4.8 km (+35%) and 28.8 min against 20.5 min, so the driver did not take the shortest and most direct route.
  • There is exactly one off-route segment, 21:39–21:49, where 6.27 km was driven over ground the optimal route covers in 1.71 km, adding 4.56 km and reaching up to 1958 m off the optimal route.
Driver's advocate
  • The only off-route segment is a single continuous run 21:39–21:49 adding 4.56 km (F3); the alleged stop was proposed by the driver before any deviation and agreed to by the rider at 21:36, before the segment began at 21:39 (F8, F9).
  • The rider's own message 'Sure, no problem.' at 21:36 (F9) answers an ask that named no road and no destination, only 'the petrol station just ahead' (F8), so the consent given was to a stop, and the record contains no fact tying the 21:39–21:49 segment to any named road; on a strict reading this is a scope question the judge may find undetermined rather than an unexcused detour (F3, F8, F9).
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 21:39:55 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F8: Chat #1 21:35 driver: “Mind if I pull in at the petrol station just ahead for one minute? Tank is almost empty.”; F9: Chat #2 21:36 rider: “Sure, no problem.”; F11: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “The rider agreed to a short stop so I thought a small change of route was fine.” (F8, F9, F11)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F8, F9)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F17)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4100/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance.

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the judge found no rider agreement covering this segment, so $6.43 is refunded; a rider message agreeing to it before it started would have exempted it.
  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.

Route deviation · B-0049

Detour · pickup chat contains a threat hint word (ordinary message)

24.5 s
Dispute statement

The app estimated 11 km; the driver turned off the route without a word and the meter read 13 km at the end. Please refund the extra distance.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Orchard Road → NUS, estimate 10.7 km / 17.3 min, pickup 09:43 drop-off 10:03; charged $29.11 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Partial refund $2.74

Issue a $2.74 Ryde voucher to the rider for the unexempted detour distance on the 09:47–09:53 off-route segment, per NS-7; no account flag.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 13.1 km vs optimal route 10.7 km: +2.4 km (+23%).
  • F2 Trip duration 20.2 min vs estimated 17.3 min (+2.9 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 09:47–09:53: 3.83 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.89 km, adding 1.94 km (up to 777 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $3.44 (2.44 km × $1.4101/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F6 Total charged $29.11 SGD: base $3.90, distance $18.50 (13.12 km), time $5.24 (20.2 min), waiting $0.00, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F7 Chat #1 09:35 rider: “I'll find you at the kerb outside Mandarin Gallery, I am in a red jacket.”
  • F8 Chat record: 1 message(s), every one timestamped.
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The trip is a metered RydeTAXI service, so the 13.1 km actually driven is billed by the meter at this trip's own per-km distance rate, which the fare fact fixes at $1.4101/km (F4, F6).
  • The GPS record shows one off-route segment, 09:47–09:53, in which 3.83 km was driven where the optimal route covers 1.89 km, adding 1.94 km and straying up to 777 m from the optimal route (F3); the trip as a whole ran 13.1 km against an optimal 10.7 km (F1), and the ride itself is 1.56 km shorter than the distance billed on top of the estimate.
Driver's advocate
  • This is a RydeTAXI metered trip: the total of $29.11 is built from base $3.90 + 13.12 km of metered distance $18.50 + 20.2 min of metered time $5.24 + platform $1.47, with waiting $0.00 and no surge, so any refund can only be the metered value of a route segment found unjustified.
  • The record shows one off-route segment only, 09:47–09:53, in which the car drove 3.83 km where the optimal route covers 1.89 km, adding 1.94 km; the rider's reference to an '11 km' estimate is not a record, the record figure being 10.7 km.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 09:47:50 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F7: Chat #1 09:35 rider: “I'll find you at the kerb outside Mandarin Gallery, I am in a red jacket.”; F9: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “I followed my GPS; it rerouted me. Nothing wrong with the fare.” (F7, F9)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F7)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F15)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. hint word in F7 - a locator, not a finding; read it in context (F7)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4101/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance.

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the judge found no rider agreement covering this segment, so $2.74 is refunded; a rider message agreeing to it before it started would have exempted it.
  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.
Notices
Notice to the rider
We reviewed your dispute and we're upholding it in part. Your trip showed one stretch, about 09:47 to 09:53, where the route taken was longer than needed - roughly 1.94 km of extra distance. We found nothing in your messages or the trip details that justified that detour, so we're refunding it. You'll get a $2.74 Ryde voucher for that extra distance. Your account is not being flagged and no further action is needed from you.
Notice to the driver

The GPS record shows one stretch, about 09:47 to 09:53, where the route taken was longer than the shortest reasonable route, adding about 1.94 km. Nothing in the trip record exempts that detour. What each side argued
Rider's advocate

Driver's advocate
  • The only off-route segment in the record is F3 (13:07–13:19, 7.41 km driven where the optimal route covers 2.38 km, +5.03 km), and it began after, not before, the rider's message F9 of 13:04.
  • The driver proposed leaving the optimal route before the segment started (F8, 13:03: 'Traffic ahead on Ayer Rajah Expressway is building up, shall I take another way?') and the rider's own reply F9 (13:04: 'Just take whichever way is faster lah, I'm late.') leaves the route choice to the driver, which is a general route authorisation and not consent to any named road.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 13:07:15 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F8: Chat #1 13:03 driver: “Traffic ahead on Ayer Rajah Expressway is building up, shall I take another way?”; F9: Chat #2 13:04 rider: “Just take whichever way is faster lah, I'm late.”; F11: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “Traffic was building up ahead. I asked the rider and the rider said any faster way was fine, so I went round.” (F8, F9, F11)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F8, F9)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F18)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4101/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False

  • Before that segment began, the driver asked at 21:21 to go round via Ang Mo Kio Avenue 5 and the rider answered at 21:22 'Sure, no problem' - a rider-issued agreement to a named road made before the segment start (F3 starts 21:25), so RD-5 is the applicable test for that segment rather than 'consent after the fact'.
Driver's advocate
  • The trip is a non-metered RydeX fixed-quote booking: the fare was quoted at booking, billed distance 17.6 km equals the optimal route distance, so no distance-based overcharge exists (F4, F6); the only refundable items on a non-metered service are unpublished add-ons or tolls attributable to a non-exempt segment (RD-2).
  • There is exactly one off-route segment, 21:25–21:32, 4.46 km driven where the optimal route covers 2.34 km, adding 2.12 km (F3); everything else in the +2.9 km total (F1) is corridor noise not attributable to any segment and is not refundable under RD-2.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 21:25:10 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes no amount (fixed fare) and no flag: if you cannot settle it, undetermined is allowed and the case may still be ruled. Evidence - F7: Chat #1 21:21 driver: “Traffic ahead on Central Expressway is building up, ok if I go round via Ang Mo Kio Avenue 5?”; F8: Chat #2 21:22 rider: “Sure, no problem.”; F10: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “Traffic was building up ahead so I asked the rider before turning off via Ang Mo Kio Avenue 5 and the rider agreed.” (F7, F8, F10)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F7, F8)
  • ✗ RD-2 metered service (detour km enters the fare) [case] RydeX is a fixed fare (no evidence)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F17)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: no_action, escalate
  • flag_account required: False
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. No refund is due.

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the record does not settle whether the rider's agreement covers this segment; a person decides.
  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.

We looked at your route-deviation complaint and there is nothing to refund. Your RydeX fare was a fixed quote of $36.95 for 17.6 km, which is the same as the best route, so the fare was billed correctly and no tolls or extra charges were added. Your trip did include one stretch where the driver went further than needed, and we could not confirm whether the route you agreed to in chat covered that stretch, so we are recording that part as undetermined in the file. No refund is due and no action is taken on the driver's account.
Notice to the driver
We reviewed the route-deviation complaint on your RydeX trip. The fare was a fixed $36.95 quote billed at 17.6 km, the best route distance, with no tolls or add-ons, so no refund applies and there is no change to your account. One stretch of the trip does not match the best route, and the record does not show which roads you drove there, so we could not confirm it was covered by the route the rider agreed to in chat. We are recording that stretch as undetermined in the file. Nothing further is required from you.

Route deviation · B-0063

Detour · fixed fare consent ns

26.7 s
Dispute statement

The driver took a longer way round than the app showed. The fare was a fixed quote, but I want the detour looked at.

Trip summary

RydeX NUS → Suntec City, estimate 14.0 km / 22.9 min, pickup 22:21 drop-off 22:52; charged $30.99 (fixed)

Proposed outcome

No refund

Close with no refund; note in the trip record that the driver's shortest-and-most-direct duty (RD-1) was breached on the 4.08 km off-route segment but no recoverable item exists on a fixed-quote RydeX fare.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 18.5 km vs optimal route 14.0 km: +4.5 km (+33%).
  • F2 Trip duration 31.1 min vs estimated 22.9 min (+8.2 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 22:33–22:42: 5.74 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.66 km, adding 4.08 km (up to 1450 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Billed distance 14.0 km does not exceed the optimal route (14.0 km).
  • F5 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F6 Total charged $30.99 SGD: base $3.90, distance $19.67 (13.95 km), time $5.95 (22.9 min), waiting $0.00, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F7 Chat #1 22:29 driver: “Jam on Central Expressway is building up ahead, can I go round via Lower Delta Road?”
  • F8 Chat #2 22:29 rider: “Sure, go ahead.”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The GPS track shows one off-route segment, 22:33–22:42, in which 5.74 km was driven where the optimal route covers 1.66 km, adding 4.08 km and straying up to 1450 m from the optimal route (F3); overall the trip ran 18.5 km against a 14.0 km optimal route, +4.5 km (+33%), and took 31.1 min against 22.9 min (F1, F2).
  • The rider's only message before the segment was 'Sure, go ahead' (F8, 22:29) answering the driver's question 'Jam on Central Expressway is building up ahead, can I go round via Lower Delta Road?' (F7, 22:29); the yes is a timing agreement to a named road, so consent reaches only driving on Lower Delta Road, and it is not a general 'whichever way is faster' authorisation (F7, F8).
Driver's advocate
  • The only off-route segment on the record is F3 (22:33–22:42, 5.74 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.66 km, +4.08 km), and it began at 22:33 - four minutes after the rider's own message F8 ('Sure, go ahead.') timestamped 22:29, so the rider's agreement precedes the segment.
  • The driver asked before leaving the optimal route and named the road to be taken: F7 at 22:29 - 'Jam on Central Expressway is building up ahead, can I go round via Lower Delta Road?' - and the rider's reply was an unqualified yes (F8, 'Sure, go ahead.'), which is an agreement to that named road and nothing wider.

Notice to the driver
We reviewed the off-route segment on your RydeX trip and are closing the case with no refund and no action against your account.

Two facts decided this. The fare was a fixed quote, so the extra distance did not add anything to the rider's bill, and nothing recoverable was charged on that stretch. There was no traffic incident on the route at the time, so the detour is not treated as an exempt one, and we have noted the longer route in the trip record.

No amount is deducted or paid, and your account is not flagged.
